So we need the fundamental theorem at the body typed... but the abs is typed T1 -> T2 in the T_Abs case, so T0 = T1 ✓. But in the generic R properties (like R_app), we may have R (T1->T2) u where u is an abs with a different annotation? Let's see: R_app: R (T1->T2) u and u -->* abs T body. The annotation T could be anything (since R's definition doesn't constrain it). Then from V (arrow) at u, we get for w: R T2 (app (abs T body) w). To get SN (open body w) from app (abs T body) w --> open body w, we need R T2 (open body w) — but R T2 (app abs w) only gives information about value reducts of app abs w, and open body w is such a reduct only via the beta step; then R T2 (open body w) follows by R_multi forward closure ✓ — no typing of body needed! Great: R's clause is defined semantically and works for any abs.

But then the type annotation T in `abs T body` is irrelevant to R ✓ (except beta ignores types too: ST_AppAbs has `forall T t v, ... tm_app (tm_abs T t) v --> open t v`, no relation between T and the typing. ✓).

Lemma value_multi_eq : forall t t', multi step t t' -> value t -> t' = t.
Proof.

  - exfalso. eapply value_no_step; eassumption.

Lemma sn_app : forall u, locally_closed u -> strongly_normalizing u ->
  forall v, locally_closed v -> strongly_normalizing v ->

Now the fundamental theorem. Let me write the context environment relation:

```
Definition env_rel (Gamma : context) (sigma : atom -> tm) : Prop :=
  (forall x, locally_closed (sigma x)) /\
  (forall x T, Gamma x = Some T -> R T (sigma x)).
```

Fundamental theorem:
```
Lemma fundamental : forall Gamma t T, has_type Gamma t T ->
  forall sigma, env_rel Gamma sigma -> R T (subst sigma t).
Proof.
  induction 1 as [...]; intros sigma [Hlc_sig Hsem].
```
The induction principle of has_type has premises:
- T_Var : forall Gamma x T, Gamma x = Some T -> has_type Gamma (tm_fvar x) T.
- T_Abs : forall (L : list atom) Gamma T1 T2 t1, (forall x, ~ In x L -> has_type (update Gamma x T1) (open t1 (fvar x)) T2) -> has_type Gamma (tm_abs T1 t1) (T1 -> T2).
- T_App : ...
- T_True, T_False.

The premise is `forall x, ~ In x L -> has_type ...`, which is a product (Pi) and arrow; Coq's induction on the derivation will generate an IH `forall x, ~ In x L -> P (update Gamma x T1) (open t1 (fvar x)) T2` if P is the motive. I need to define the motive as `fun Gamma t T => forall sigma, env_rel Gamma sigma -> R T (subst sigma t)`. Then the induction gives exactly that.

Hmm, but the T_Abs case has a recursive has_type occurrence under a binder `forall x, ~ In x L -> has_type ...`. Coq's induction principle does handle this: it gives `forall x, ~ In x L -> IH` where IH is the motive at the subderivation. ✓ This is standard (SF uses this pattern).

Theorem normalization : forall t T,
  <{ empty |-- t \in T }> ->
  strongly_normalizing t.
Proof.
  intros t T Hty.
  pose proof (fundamental empty t T Hty (fun x => tm_fvar x) ltac:(split; [intros; constructor|intros x S H; simpl in H; discriminate H])) as HR.
  (* HR : R T (subst (fun x => fvar x) t) *)
  rewrite subst_id in HR.
  eapply R_sn; eassumption.
Qed.
```

Let me write:

```coq
(* ==================== Auxiliary definitions and lemmas ==================== *)

(** A fresh atom for a finite list of atoms. *)

Fixpoint max_list (L : list atom) : atom :=
  match L with
  | nil => 0
  | x :: L' => Nat.max x (max_list L')
  end.

Lemma max_list_spec : forall (L : list atom) (x : atom),
  In x L -> x <= max_list L.
Proof.
  induction L as [| a L' IH]; intros x Hin.
  - destruct Hin.
  - destruct Hin as [-> | Hin].
    + apply Nat.le_max_l.
    + eapply Nat.le_trans; [apply IH; exact Hin | apply Nat.le_max_r].
Qed.

Definition fresh (L : list atom) : atom := S (max_list L).

Lemma fresh_notin : forall L : list atom, ~ In (fresh L) L.
Proof.
  intros L Hin. unfold fresh in Hin.
  pose proof (max_list_spec L _ Hin). lia.
Qed.
```

Requires `lia` — from Stdlib Require Import Arith.PeanoNat gives `Nat.le_*` but `lia` is in `Lia` (Stdlib.micromega.Lia). Let me add `From Stdlib Require Import Lia.` at the top of my additions (allowed, it's a library import, not modifying definitions). Actually, the file already imports `Arith.PeanoNat`; `lia` needs `Lia`. I can add the import after the existing ones. That's fine.

Alternatively avoid lia: `max_list_spec` gives x <= max_list L, so S(max_list L) ≠ x by `Nat.lt_neq`? x <= m < S m. `Nat.lt_neq` : forall n m, n < m -> n <> m. Use `apply Nat.lt_neq; apply Nat.lt_succ_r? ...` hmm: x <= m → x < S m by `Nat.lt_succ_r`? Let me just use lia; it's a standard library.

fv:
```coq
Fixpoint fv (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => nil
  | tm_fvar x => x :: nil
  | tm_app t1 t2 => fv t1 ++ fv t2
  | tm_abs _ t1 => fv t1
  | tm_true => nil
  | tm_false => nil
  end.
```

subst:
```coq
Fixpoint subst (sigma : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => sigma x
  | tm_app t1 t2 => tm_app (subst sigma t1) (subst sigma t2)
  | tm_abs T t1 => tm_abs T (subst sigma t1)
  | tm_true => tm_true
  | tm_false => tm_false
  end.

Definition extend (x : atom) (u : tm) (sigma : atom -> tm) : atom -> tm :=
  fun y => if Nat.eqb x y then u else sigma y.

Lemma subst_lc : forall t k sigma,
  lc_at k t -> (forall x, locally_closed (sigma x)) -> lc_at k (subst sigma t).

  - apply Hsig.
  - inversion Hlc; subst. constructor; auto.
  - inversion Hlc; subst. constructor. apply IHt. assumption. assumption.

  - constructor.
Qed.

Lemma value_multi_eq : forall t t', multi step t t' -> value t -> t' = t.

Qed.
```

The goal after induction in the step case would be `value t -> t' = t` where the step is t --> b. We need to prove t' = t from value t, but value t contradicts the step ✓. But wait, is the IH even usable? Not needed. `intro Hv; exfalso; eapply value_no_step; eassumption` ✓.

Careful about the naming of the constructor arguments in induction: `induction Hm as [| a b c Hstep Hrest IH]` — the multi_step constructor is `multi_step x y z : R x y -> multi R y z -> multi R x z`, so the pattern gives a, b, c, Hstep, Hrest, IH ✓ (6 names? the constructor has 6 arguments: x, y, z, the relation proof, the multi proof; plus IH). Hmm, but with `induction Hm`, does Coq substitute the endpoints in the goal? The goal is `value t -> t' = t`. Since t and t' are the endpoints of Hm, Coq should abstract them. Hmm, actually the constructor's arguments: multi_step : forall x y z, R x y -> multi R y z -> multi R x z, so with Hm : multi step t t', matching gives x = t, z = t', y = b. ```
Lemma multi_trans : forall t1 t2 t3, multi step t1 t2 -> multi step t2 t3 -> multi step t1 t3.
Proof.
  intros t1 t2 t3 H12 H23. revert t3 H23. induction H12 as [| a b c Hstep Hrest IH]; intros t3 H23.
  - exact H23.
  - eapply multi_step; [exact Hstep | apply IH; exact H23].
Qed.
```
In the step case: H12 = multi_step a b c Hstep Hrest where a = t1, c = t2; IH : forall t3, multi step b t3 -> multi step b t3? Hmm, IH should be `forall t3, multi step c t3 -> multi step b t3`? No wait. Let me think: H12 : multi step t1 t2. Induction on it: refl case t1 = t2, goal `multi step t2 t3 -> multi step t1 t3` → exact H23 after subst ✓. Step case: t1 --> b and multi b t2. IH : forall t3, multi step b t3 -> multi step b... no: the IH is the motive at the sub-derivation: `forall t3, multi step t2' t3 -> multi step b t3`? Hmm.

Motive: P(t1, t2, H12) := forall t3, multi step t2 t3 -> multi step t1 t3.
Step case: H12 = multi_step t1 b t2 Hstep Hrest (with Hrest : multi step b t2). IH := P(b, t2, Hrest) = forall t3, multi step t2 t3 -> multi step b t3.
Goal: forall t3, multi step t2 t3 -> multi step t1 t3.
Given H23 : multi step t2 t3. Apply multi_step t1 b t3 Hstep (IH t3 H23) ✓.
So `eapply multi_step; [exact Hstep | apply IH; exact H23]` ✓.

Now the R definition:

```coq
Fixpoint R (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  locally_closed t /\
  strongly_normalizing t /\
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool =>
      forall v, multi step t v -> value v -> (v = tm_true \/ v = tm_false)
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      forall v, multi step t v -> value v ->
        (forall w, value w -> R T1 w -> R T2 (tm_app v w))
  end.
```

Hmm, a subtlety: Coq might complain that R is not structurally recursive because the recursive calls occur inside a `match` on T that's inside a conjunction. It should be fine: R T1 w and R T2 (app v w) with T1, T2 subterms of T = Ty_Arrow T1 T2 ✓.

Another concern: the match on T returns a Prop; T is the fixpoint's structural argument ✓.

Proof. intros T t H. destruct T; simpl in H; tauto. Qed.
```
Hmm, `simpl in H` — since R is a Fixpoint and T is now a concrete constructor (Ty_Bool or Ty_Arrow T1 T2), simpl should reduce R. But note R's body is `locally_closed t /\ strongly_normalizing t /\ match T with ... end`. After destruct T, `simpl in H` should beta-reduce the match ✓. Then H : lc t /\ SN t /\ (...). tauto proves lc t ✓.

Wait, but does `destruct T` require the goal to be generalized? Goal `locally_closed t` doesn't mention T ✓. But H does; destruct T replaces T with constructors in H ✓.

R_multi:
```coq
Lemma R_multi : forall T t t', R T t -> multi step t t' -> R T t'.
Proof.
  intros T t t' HR Hm. destruct T; simpl in *.
  - destruct HR as [Hlc [Hsn Hcl]]. split; [|split].
    + eapply multi_preserves_lc; eassumption.
    + eapply SN_multi; eassumption.
    + intros v Hmv Hval. eapply Hcl; [|exact Hval]. eapply multi_trans; eassumption.
  - destruct HR as [Hlc [Hsn Hcl]]. repeat split.
    + eapply multi_preserves_lc; eassumption.
    + eapply SN_multi; eassumption.
    + intros v Hmv Hval w Hwval HRw. eapply Hcl; [|exact Hval|exact Hwval|exact HRw]. eapply multi_trans; eassumption.
Qed.
```
Careful: after `destruct T; simpl in *`, in the arrow case T is replaced by `Ty_Arrow T1 T2` where T1, T2 are the destructed args; the goal is about R T1 w etc. ✓. `repeat split` might be problematic for the arrow case: goal is `lc t' /\ SN t' /\ (forall ...)`. repeat split would split the forall? No, split applies to conjunctions; repeat split splits the two conjunctions into 3 subgoals ✓ (it stops at the forall goal since split doesn't apply).

Hmm, but wait: in the arrow case, R T t' simplifies to `lc t' /\ SN t' /\ (forall v, multi step t' v -> value v -> forall w, value w -> R T1 w -> R T2 (app v w))`. ✓.

Now app_multi_value_inv:

```coq
Lemma value_app_absurd : forall t1 t2, value (tm_app t1 t2) -> False.
Proof. intros t1 t2 H; inversion H. Qed.

Lemma app_multi_value_inv : forall u v z,
  multi step (tm_app u v) z -> value z ->
  exists (T : ty) (body v' : tm),
    multi step u (tm_abs T body) /\ multi step v v' /\ value v' /\ multi step (open body v') z.
Proof.
  intros u v z Hm. revert u v.
  induction Hm as [| x y z' Hstep Hrest IH]; intros u v Heq Hval.
  - (* refl *) subst x. exfalso. eapply value_app_absurd; eassumption.
```

No: `revert u v` reverts the variables, giving `forall u v, multi step (tm_app u v) z -> value z -> exists ...` as the goal; then `induction Hm` — but Hm still references u and v which are now reverted, so Hm's statement would be... Hmm, you can't revert u and v if Hm depends on them (the revert would fail because Hm mentions them? Actually revert only affects the goal; Hm stays in the context referencing u and v — but u, v no longer being in the context is a problem: revert moves them into the goal, but Hm still depends on them, so Coq would complain "u is used in hypothesis Hm"). Yes, `revert u` fails if Hm depends on u.

Standard approach: `remember (tm_app u v) as t eqn:E.` then `revert u v E`. Then induction on Hm.

```
Lemma app_multi_value_inv : forall u v z,
  multi step (tm_app u v) z -> value z ->
  exists T body v', multi step u (tm_abs T body) /\ multi step v v' /\ value v' /\ multi step (open body v') z.
Proof.
  intros u v z Hm. remember (tm_app u v) as t eqn:E. revert u v E.
  induction Hm as [| a b c Hstep Hrest IH]; intros u v E Hval.
```
Hmm, the order: after `remember`, Hm : multi step t z with E : t = tm_app u v. `revert u v E` gives goal `forall u v, t = tm_app u v -> value z -> exists ...`. Then `induction Hm` — but Hm still mentions t and z, and also... the goal mentions t. The induction motive: `fun (a c : tm) (h : multi step a c) => forall u v, a = tm_app u v -> value c -> exists ...`. Hmm, does Coq generate this? With `induction Hm`, the motive is derived from the goal by abstracting t and z (and Hm). The goal is `forall u v, t = tm_app u v -> value z -> exists ...`; the occurrence of `z` is inside `value z` and the conclusion; it will be abstracted ✓.

So after induction:
- refl case: a = c, Hm = multi_refl a. Goal: forall u v, a = tm_app u v -> value a -> exists ... . Intros u v E Hval. subst a. Now E : tm_app u v = tm_app u v (or something) and Hval : value (tm_app u v). We need `exfalso; eapply value_app_absurd; eassumption` ✓. But careful: after subst, the goal is `exists ...` with Hval : value (tm_app u v)? Hmm, `subst a` where E : a = tm_app u v — substituting a := tm_app u v in the goal and Hval ✓.

- step case: a --> b, Hrest : multi step b c. IH : forall u v, b = tm_app u v -> value c -> exists ... (for the full IH we need to know its exact form; likely `IH : forall u v, b = tm_app u v -> value c -> exists ...`). Goal: forall u v, a = tm_app u v -> value c -> exists ...
  Intros u v E Hval. subst a (E : a = tm_app u v → rewrite). Now Hstep : tm_app u v --> b. Inversion on Hstep:
  + ST_AppAbs: gives u = abs T t0, v = v0, b = open t0 v0. Goal: exists T body v', multi u (abs T body) /\ ... 
    Take T, t0, v0: multi u (abs T t0) = multi_refl u (after subst u := abs T t0 ✓), multi v v0 = multi_refl ✓, value v0 ✓ from the rule's premise, and multi (open t0 v0) c = Hrest ✓.
  + ST_App1: gives u --> t1', v = t2, b = app t1' t2. Apply IH with u := t1', v := t2: need b = tm_app t1' t2 ✓ (reflexivity) and Hval ✓. Then sn_app_value:
```coq
Lemma sn_app_value : forall v, strongly_normalizing v ->
  forall u, value u -> locally_closed v ->
  (forall (T : ty) (body v' : tm),
      multi step u (tm_abs T body) -> multi step v v' -> value v' ->
      strongly_normalizing (open body v')) ->
  strongly_normalizing (tm_app u v).
Proof.
  intros v Hsn. induction Hsn as [v Hred IH].
  intros u Huval Hlcv Hcompat.
  apply SN_intro. intros t' Hstep.
  inversion Hstep as [T0 body w Hlcabs Hvw | t1 t1' t2 Hst1 Hlc2 | v1 t2 t2' Hval1 Hst2]; subst.
  - eapply Hcompat; [apply multi_refl | apply multi_refl | exact Hvw].
  - exfalso. eapply value_no_step; eassumption.
  - eapply IH; [exact Hst2 | exact Huval | eapply step_preserves_lc; eassumption |].
    intros T body v' Hmu Hmv' Hvv'. eapply Hcompat; [exact Hmu | | exact Hvv'].
    eapply multi_step; eassumption.
Qed.
```
Let me check the details:
- After `induction Hsn as [v Hred IH]`: the motive is `fun v => forall u, value u -> lc v -> compat u v -> SN (app u v)`. Hmm, careful: the motive includes `v` in Hlcv and compat; after abstraction the motive is `fun y => forall u, value u -> locally_closed y -> (forall T body v', multi step u (tm_abs T body) -> multi step y v' -> value v' -> SN (open body v')) -> SN (app u y)` ✓. So the IH should be exactly that ✓.

- `inversion Hstep as [...]`: Hstep : tm_app u v --> t'. 
  Constructor patterns:
  - ST_AppAbs : forall T t v0, locally_closed (tm_abs T t) -> value v0 -> tm_app (tm_abs T t) v0 --> open t v0. Pattern names: T t v0 Hlcabs Hv0. But since our term is tm_app u v, inversion gives u = tm_abs T t and v = v0 and t' = open t v0.
  - ST_App1 : forall t1 t1' t2, t1 --> t1' -> locally_closed t2 -> tm_app t1 t2 --> tm_app t1' t2. Names: t1 t1' t2 Hst1 Hlc2.
  - ST_App2 : forall v1 t2 t2', value v1 -> t2 --> t2' -> tm_app v1 t2 --> tm_app v1 t2'. Names: v1 t2 t2' Hval1 Hst2.
  With `subst`, u and v get replaced everywhere.

  Case 1: goal SN (open t v0)?? Actually t' = open t v0; after subst, the goal is SN (open t v0) and Hcompat needs multi u (tm_abs T t) — with u = tm_abs T t (after subst), multi_refl ✓; multi v v0 with v = v0 → multi_refl ✓; value v0 ✓.
  Hmm, but wait: `Hcompat` was defined with `u` before the substitution; after subst u := tm_abs T t, Hcompat becomes `forall T body v', multi step (tm_abs T t) (tm_abs T' body) -> ...`. Applying with T := T, body := t, v' := v0: multi (abs T t) (abs T t) ✓ multi_refl; multi v0 v0 ✓; value v0 ✓. 
  
  Case 2: ST_App1: u --> t1' — but Huval : value u, contradiction with value_no_step ✓.
  
  Case 3: ST_App2: v --> t2', goal SN (app u t2'). Use IH: IH y Hst2 u Huval (lc t2') compat' where lc t2' from lc v and step_preserves_lc ✓ and compat' : forall T body v', multi u (abs T body) -> multi t2' v' -> value v' -> SN (open body v'). Given those, derive multi v v' via multi_step v t2' Hst2 Hmv' ✓ then Hcompat ✓.

  Careful: after inversion+subst, the variables v and u may have been renamed/replaced. E.g., in case 3, v = t2 and u = v1. The goal is SN (app v1 t2'), IH at v := t2 etc. Should work after subst.

Potential issue: `inversion Hstep` might produce extra equalities like `tm_app u v = tm_app (tm_abs T t) v0`; `subst` should handle them. If inversion doesn't substitute cleanly, I may need `injection`. I'll iterate.

Then sn_app:
```coq
Lemma sn_app : forall u, locally_closed u -> strongly_normalizing u ->
  forall v, locally_closed v -> strongly_normalizing v ->
  (forall (T : ty) (body v' : tm),
      multi step u (tm_abs T body) -> multi step v v' -> value v' ->
      strongly_normalizing (open body v')) ->
  strongly_normalizing (tm_app u v).
Proof.
  intros u Hlcu Hsn. induction Hsn as [u Hred IH].
  intros v Hlcv Hsnv Hcompat.
  apply SN_intro. intros t' Hstep.
  inversion Hstep as [T0 body w Hlcabs Hvw | t1 t1' t2 Hst1 Hlc2 | v1 t2 t2' Hval1 Hst2]; subst.
  - eapply Hcompat; [apply multi_refl | apply multi_refl | exact Hvw].
  - eapply IH; [exact Hst1 | exact Hlcv | exact Hsnv |].
    intros T body v' Hmu Hmv' Hvv'. eapply Hcompat; [|exact Hmv'|exact Hvv']. eapply multi_step; eassumption.
  - eapply sn_app_value; [exact Hval1 | eapply step_preserves_lc; eassumption | eapply SN_step; eassumption |].
    intros T body v' Hmu Hmv' Hvv'. eapply Hcompat; [exact Hmu | | exact Hvv']. eapply multi_step; eassumption.
Qed.
```

Now, the fundamental theorem. Let me write it:

```coq
Definition env_rel (Gamma : context) (sigma : atom -> tm) : Prop :=
  (forall x, locally_closed (sigma x)) /\
  (forall (x : atom) (T : ty), Gamma x = Some T -> R T (sigma x)).

Lemma fundamental : forall Gamma t T, has_type Gamma t T ->
  forall sigma, env_rel Gamma sigma -> R T (subst sigma t).
Proof.
  induction 1 as [Gamma x T Hlookup
               | L Gamma T1 T2 t1 Hprem IH
               | Gamma t1 t2 T1 T2 Ht1 IH1 Ht2 IH2
               | Gamma
               | Gamma]; intros sigma [Hlcsig Hsem].
```
Hmm, the induction principle's case names: with `induction 1 as [...]`, the patterns correspond to the constructors' arguments:
- T_Var : forall Gamma x T, Gamma x = Some T -> has_type Gamma (tm_fvar x) T. Args: Gamma x T Hlookup. Pattern: `Gamma x T Hlookup`. But wait, the induction principle will have the IHs? No premises with has_type, so no IHs.
- T_Abs : forall (L : list atom) Gamma T1 T2 t1, (forall x, ~ In x L -> has_type ...) -> has_type Gamma (tm_abs T1 t1) (T1 -> T2). Args: L, Gamma, T1, T2, t1, the premise (with a Pi/arrow). The pattern gives names for L, Gamma, T1, T2, t1, and then the IH. Hmm, the premise is `forall x, ~ In x L -> has_type ...` — Coq's induction will treat this as one argument (a function) and give the IH inside? Actually, the generated induction principle for a constructor with a premise of the form `forall x, ~ In x L -> has_type ...` is:
  ```
  (forall L Gamma T1 T2 t1, (forall x, ~ In x L -> has_type (update Gamma x T1) (open t1 (fvar x)) T2) ->
     (forall x, ~ In x L -> P (update Gamma x T1) (open t1 (fvar x)) T2) ->
     P Gamma (tm_abs T1 t1) (T1 -> T2))
  ```
  Wait, how does Coq handle nested Pi types in the constructor arguments? Coq's induction principle generation treats the constructor's type: `forall (L : list atom) Gamma T1 T2 t1, (forall x, ~ In x L -> has_type ...) -> has_type Gamma (tm_abs T1 t1) (T1->T2)`. The premise `forall x, ~ In x L -> has_type ...` is an argument (a function). For the induction principle, Coq generates a hypothesis for this argument AND an IH for each recursive occurrence of has_type in it. The recursive occurrence is under the binders `forall x, ~ In x L ->`, so the IH is `forall x, ~ In x L -> P (update Gamma x T1) (open t1 (fvar x)) T2`. 
  
  So the pattern for `induction 1 as [...]` in the T_Abs case: the arguments are L, Gamma, T1, T2, t1, Hprem (the premise function), IH. So the pattern is `L Gamma T1 T2 t1 Hprem IH` ✓.
  
- T_App : forall Gamma t1 t2 T1 T2, has_type Gamma t1 (T1->T2) -> has_type Gamma t2 T1 -> has_type Gamma (app t1 t2) T2. Args: Gamma t1 t2 T1 T2 H1 IH1 H2 IH2.
- T_True : forall Gamma, has_type Gamma tm_true Ty_Bool. Args: Gamma.
- T_False : forall Gamma. Args: Gamma.

So: `induction 1 as [Gamma x T Hlookup | L Gamma T1 T2 t1 Hprem IH | Gamma t1 t2 T1 T2 Ht1 IH1 Ht2 IH2 | Gamma | Gamma]; intros sigma [Hlcsig Hsem].`

Hmm, `induction 1` requires the goal to be `forall sigma, env_rel Gamma sigma -> R T (subst sigma t)`. With `induction 1` on H : has_type Gamma t T, Coq abstracts Gamma, t, T. ✓ But careful: the goal mentions t via `subst sigma t`; the abstraction should work.

Now the final theorem. Also the has_type_lc lemma.

Let me write has_type_lc:
```coq
Lemma has_type_lc : forall Gamma t T, has_type Gamma t T -> locally_closed t.
Proof.
  intros Gamma t T H. induction H as [Gamma x T Hlookup
    | L Gamma T1 T2 t1 Hprem IH
    | Gamma t1 t2 T1 T2 Ht1 IH1 Ht2 IH2
    | Gamma | Gamma].
  - (* T_Var *) apply lc_fvar.
  - (* T_Abs *) apply lc_abs.
    set (x := fresh (L ++ fv t1)).
    assert (HxL : ~ In x L) by (unfold x; intro Hin; apply fresh_notin with (L := L ++ fv t1); rewrite in_app_iff; left; exact Hin).
    pose proof (IH x HxL) as Hlc.
    apply lc_open_fvar_inv with (x := x).
    exact Hlc.
  - (* T_App *) apply lc_app; assumption.
  - apply lc_true.
  - apply lc_false.
Qed.
```

```coq
Lemma subst_lc : forall t k sigma,
  lc_at k t ->
  (forall x, locally_closed (sigma x)) ->
  lc_at k (subst sigma t).
Proof.
  intros t. induction t; intros k sigma Hlc Hsig; simpl.
  - (* bvar *) inversion Hlc as [kk i Hi | | | | |]; subst. constructor. exact Hi.
  - (* fvar *) apply lc_at_mono with (k := 0); [apply Hsig | lia].
  - (* app *) inversion Hlc as [| | kk t1 t2 H1 H2 | | |]; subst. constructor; [apply IHt1 | apply IHt2]; assumption.
  - (* abs *) inversion Hlc as [| | | kk T0 t1 H1 | |]; subst. constructor. apply IHt1 with (k := S kk); assumption.
  - constructor.
  - constructor.
Qed.
```

- lc_fvar : forall k x, lc_at k (tm_fvar x) → [k x]

- lc_false : forall k → [k]

```coq
(* ---------- fresh atoms ---------- *)

Fixpoint max_list (L : list atom) : atom :=
  match L with
  | nil => 0
  | x :: L' => Nat.max x (max_list L')
  end.

Lemma max_list_spec : forall (L : list atom) (x : atom),
  In x L -> x <= max_list L.
Proof.
  induction L as [| a L' IH]; intros x Hin.
  - destruct Hin.
  - destruct Hin as [-> | Hin].
    + apply Nat.le_max_l.
    + eapply Nat.le_trans; [apply IH; exact Hin | apply Nat.le_max_r].
Qed.

Definition fresh (L : list atom) : atom := S (max_list L).

Lemma fresh_notin : forall L : list atom, ~ In (fresh L) L.
Proof.
  intros L Hin. unfold fresh in Hin.
  pose proof (max_list_spec L _ Hin). lia.
Qed.
```

Note: the file opens `stlc_scope`, which might affect notation parsing for numbers? `0`, `S`... The scope stlc_scope contains notations for tm. `Nat.max` arguments are nats; `0` might be interpreted... Since `0` is a numeral, it's parsed via the nat_scope by default (numerals are interpreted based on the expected type). `S (...)` is a constructor of nat ✓. Might need `%nat` annotations in some places, but S and 0 should be fine.

Careful: `fresh` uses `S` — the constructor of nat ✓.

Fixpoint fv (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => nil
  | tm_fvar x => x :: nil
  | tm_app t1 t2 => fv t1 ++ fv t2
  | tm_abs _ t1 => fv t1
  | tm_true => nil
  | tm_false => nil
  end.
```

subst:
```coq
Fixpoint subst (sigma : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => sigma x
  | tm_app t1 t2 => tm_app (subst sigma t1) (subst sigma t2)
  | tm_abs T t1 => tm_abs T (subst sigma t1)
  | tm_true => tm_true
  | tm_false => tm_false
  end.

Definition extend (x : atom) (u : tm) (sigma : atom -> tm) : atom -> tm :=
  fun y => if Nat.eqb x y then u else sigma y.
```

```coq
(* ====================================================================== *)
(** * Auxiliary development for strong normalization                      *)
(* ====================================================================== *)

(** ** Fresh atoms *)

Fixpoint max_list (L : list atom) : atom :=
  match L with
  | nil => 0
  | x :: L' => Nat.max x (max_list L')
  end.

Lemma max_list_spec : forall (L : list atom) (x : atom),
  In x L -> x <= max_list L.
Proof.
  induction L as [| a L' IH]; intros x Hin.
  - destruct Hin.
  - destruct Hin as [-> | Hin].
    + apply Nat.le_max_l.
    + eapply Nat.le_trans; [apply IH; exact Hin | apply Nat.le_max_r].
Qed.

Definition fresh (L : list atom) : atom := S (max_list L).

Lemma fresh_notin : forall L : list atom, ~ In (fresh L) L.
Proof.
  intros L Hin. unfold fresh in Hin.
  pose proof (max_list_spec L _ Hin). lia.
Qed.

(** ** Free variables *)

Fixpoint fv (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => nil
  | tm_fvar x => x :: nil
  | tm_app t1 t2 => fv t1 ++ fv t2
  | tm_abs _ t1 => fv t1
  | tm_true => nil
  | tm_false => nil
  end.

Lemma notin_fv_app_1 : forall x t1 t2, ~ In x (fv (tm_app t1 t2)) -> ~ In x (fv t1).
Proof. intros x t1 t2 H Hin. apply H. simpl. rewrite in_app_iff. left. exact Hin. Qed.

Lemma notin_fv_app_2 : forall x t1 t2, ~ In x (fv (tm_app t1 t2)) -> ~ In x (fv t2).
Proof. intros x t1 t2 H Hin. apply H. simpl. rewrite in_app_iff. right. exact Hin. Qed.

Lemma notin_fv_abs : forall x T t, ~ In x (fv (tm_abs T t)) -> ~ In x (fv t).
Proof. intros x T t H. simpl in H. exact H. Qed.

Lemma fresh_notin_app : forall L1 L2, ~ In (fresh (L1 ++ L2)) L1.
Proof.
  intros L1 L2 Hin. apply fresh_notin with (L := L1 ++ L2). rewrite in_app_iff. left. exact Hin.
Qed.

Lemma fresh_notin_app2 : forall L1 L2, ~ In (fresh (L1 ++ L2)) L2.
Proof.
  intros L1 L2 Hin. apply fresh_notin with (L := L1 ++ L2). rewrite in_app_iff. right. exact Hin.
Qed.
```

Hmm, `in_app_iff` — the Stdlib lemma name is `in_app_iff` in Lists.List ✓ (`Lemma in_app_iff : forall (A : Type) (l l' : list A) (a : A), a ∈ l ++ l' <-> a ∈ l \/ a ∈ l'`). It might require Setoid stuff? Just rewrite should work.

Now, the lc lemmas:

```coq
(** ** Basic properties of local closure *)

Lemma lc_at_mono : forall k t, lc_at k t -> forall k', k <= k' -> lc_at k' t.
Proof.
  intros k t H. induction H; intros k' Hle.
  - constructor. lia.
  - constructor.
  - constructor; [apply IHlc_at1 | apply IHlc_at2]; exact Hle.
  - constructor. apply IHlc_at. lia.
  - constructor.
  - constructor.
Qed.
```
Hmm, the IH naming: `induction H` names IHs after the premises. The premises of lc_app are lc_at k t1 and lc_at k t2; Coq names them IHlc_at1, IHlc_at2? The auto-naming uses the hypothesis name; since both premises are unnamed... Coq typically names them IHlc_at1 and IHlc_at2 ✓ I think. To be safe, I'll use `induction H as [k i Hi | k x | k t1 t2 H1 IH1 H2 IH2 | k T t1 H1 IH1 | k | k]`. Wait, the `as` pattern in induction on an inductive with premises: names for the constructor arguments including premises and IHs. For lc_app: k, t1, t2, H1 (premise), IH1, H2 (premise), IH2? The order in the induction principle: each premise is followed by its IH. Yes: `lc_at_ind : forall P, (forall k i, i < k -> P k (tm_bvar i)) -> (forall k x, P k (tm_fvar x)) -> (forall k t1 t2, lc_at k t1 -> P k t1 -> lc_at k t2 -> P k t2 -> P k (tm_app t1 t2)) -> ...`. So the pattern is [k i Hi | k x | k t1 t2 H1 IH1 H2 IH2 | k T t1 H1 IH1 | k | k] ✓.

Let me use explicit patterns to be safe.

```coq
Lemma lc_at_mono : forall k t, lc_at k t -> forall k', k <= k' -> lc_at k' t.
Proof.
  intros k t H. induction H as [k i Hi | k x | k t1 t2 H1 IH1 H2 IH2 | k T t1 H1 IH1 | k | k];
    intros k' Hle.
  - constructor. lia.
  - constructor.
  - constructor; [apply IH1 | apply IH2]; exact Hle.
  - constructor. apply IH1. lia.
  - constructor.
  - constructor.
Qed.
```

lc_open_fvar_inv:
```coq
Lemma lc_open_fvar_inv : forall t k x,
  lc_at k (open_rec k (tm_fvar x) t) -> lc_at (S k) t.
Proof.
  intro t. induction t; intros k x H; simpl in H.
  - (* bvar n *) destruct (Nat.eqb k n) eqn:E.
    + apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. subst n. constructor. lia.
    + apply Nat.eqb_neq in E. constructor. inversion H; lia.
  - (* fvar *) constructor.
  - (* app *) inversion H as [| | k' t1 t2 H1 H2 | | |]; subst. constructor; [eapply IHt1 | eapply IHt2]; eassumption.
  - (* abs *) inversion H as [| | | k' T0 t1 H1 | |]; subst. constructor. eapply IHt1. exact H1.
  - constructor.
  - constructor.
Qed.
```
Check cases:
- bvar: H : lc_at k (if k =? n then tm_fvar x else tm_bvar n). In the true case, H : lc_at k (tm_fvar x) (after subst); goal lc_at (S k) (bvar n) = lc_at (S k) (bvar k) → constructor; lia ✓. In the false case, H : lc_at k (tm_bvar n) → inversion gives n < k → goal n < S k → lia ✓.
- app: H : lc_at k (tm_app (open_rec k fx t1) (open_rec k fx t2)); inversion gives H1 : lc_at k (open_rec k fx t1), H2. Goal lc_at (S k) (app t1 t2) → constructor → IHt1 with k, x, H1 ✓.
- abs: H : lc_at k (tm_abs T (open_rec (S k) fx t1)); inversion gives lc_at (S k) (open_rec (S k) fx t1) (since lc_abs's index k' = k and premise lc_at (S k) ...). Goal lc_at (S k) (abs T t1) → constructor → lc_at (S (S k)) t1. IHt1 with k := S k, x := x, H1 : lc_at (S k) (open_rec (S k) fx t1) → lc_at (S (S k)) t1 ✓.

Now substitution lemmas:

```coq
(** ** Substitution *)

Fixpoint tsubst (sigma : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm := ...

Definition extend (x : atom) (u : tm) (sigma : atom -> tm) : atom -> tm :=
  fun y => if Nat.eqb x y then u else sigma y.

Lemma tsubst_id : forall t, tsubst (fun x => tm_fvar x) t = t.
Proof.

Let me now write the final theorem:
```coq
Theorem normalization : forall t T,
  <{ empty |-- t \in T }> ->
  strongly_normalizing t.
Proof.
  intros t T Hty.
  pose proof (fundamental empty t T Hty (fun x => tm_fvar x)) as HR.
  assert (Henv : env_rel empty (fun x => tm_fvar x)).
  { split.
    - intros x. apply lc_fvar.
    - intros x T0 Hx. unfold empty in Hx. discriminate Hx. }
  specialize (HR Henv). rewrite tsubst_id in HR.
  eapply R_sn. exact HR.
Qed.
```

Note: `empty` is defined as `fun _ => None`; `unfold empty in Hx` gives Hx : None = Some T0 → discriminate ✓.

```coq
(* ================================================================ *)
(** * Auxiliary development                                          *)
(* ================================================================ *)

(** ** Fresh atoms *)

Fixpoint max_list (L : list atom) : atom :=
  match L with
  | nil => 0
  | x :: L' => Nat.max x (max_list L')
  end.

Lemma max_list_spec : forall (L : list atom) (x : atom),
  In x L -> x <= max_list L.
Proof.
  induction L as [| a L' IH]; intros x Hin.
  - destruct Hin.
  - destruct Hin as [-> | Hin].
    + apply Nat.le_max_l.
    + eapply Nat.le_trans; [apply IH; exact Hin | apply Nat.le_max_r].
Qed.

Definition fresh (L : list atom) : atom := S (max_list L).

Lemma fresh_notin : forall L : list atom, ~ In (fresh L) L.
Proof.
  intros L Hin. unfold fresh in Hin.
  pose proof (max_list_spec L _ Hin). lia.
Qed.

Lemma fresh_notin_left : forall L1 L2 : list atom, ~ In (fresh (L1 ++ L2)) L1.
Proof.
  intros L1 L2 Hin. apply fresh_notin with (L := L1 ++ L2).
  rewrite in_app_iff. left. exact Hin.
Qed.

Lemma fresh_notin_right : forall L1 L2 : list atom, ~ In (fresh (L1 ++ L2)) L2.
Proof.
  intros L1 L2 Hin. apply fresh_notin with (L := L1 ++ L2).
  rewrite in_app_iff. right. exact Hin.
Qed.

(** ** Free variables of a term *)

Fixpoint fv (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => nil
  | tm_fvar x => x :: nil
  | tm_app t1 t2 => fv t1 ++ fv t2
  | tm_abs _ t1 => fv t1
  | tm_true => nil
  | tm_false => nil
  end.

Lemma fv_app_notin_1 : forall (x : atom) t1 t2,
  ~ In x (fv (tm_app t1 t2)) -> ~ In x (fv t1).
Proof.
  intros x t1 t2 H Hin. apply H. simpl. rewrite in_app_iff. left. exact Hin.
Qed.

Lemma fv_app_notin_2 : forall (x : atom) t1 t2,
  ~ In x (fv (tm_app t1 t2)) -> ~ In x (fv t2).
Proof.
  intros x t1 t2 H Hin. apply H. simpl. rewrite in_app_iff. right. exact Hin.
Qed.

(** ** Local closure: monotonicity and opening *)

Lemma lc_at_mono : forall k t, lc_at k t -> forall k', k <= k' -> lc_at k' t.
Proof.
  intros k t H. induction H as [k i Hi | k x | k t1 t2 H1 IH1 H2 IH2 | k T t1 H1 IH1 | k | k];
    intros k' Hle.
  - constructor. lia.
  - constructor.
  - constructor; [apply IH1 | apply IH2]; exact Hle.
  - constructor. apply IH1. lia.
  - constructor.
  - constructor.
Qed.

Lemma open_lc : forall k t, lc_at k t -> forall u, open_rec k u t = t.
Proof.
  intros k t H. induction H as [k i Hi | k x | k t1 t2 H1 IH1 H2 IH2 | k T t1 H1 IH1 | k | k];
    intros u; simpl.
  - rewrite Nat.eqb_neq by lia. reflexivity.
  - reflexivity.
  - rewrite IH1, IH2. reflexivity.
  - rewrite IH1. reflexivity.
  - reflexivity.
  - reflexivity.
Qed.

Lemma lc_open : forall t k, lc_at (S k) t -> forall u, lc_at k u -> lc_at k (open_rec k u t).
Proof.
  intro t. induction t; intros k Hlc u Hu; simpl.
  - destruct (Nat.eqb k n) eqn:E.
    + apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. subst n. exact Hu.
    + apply Nat.eqb_neq in E. constructor. inversion Hlc; subst. lia.
  - constructor.
  - inversion Hlc as [| | k' t1 t2 H1 H2 | | |]; subst.
    constructor; [eapply IHt1 | eapply IHt2]; eassumption.
  - inversion Hlc as [| | | k' T0 t1 H1 | |]; subst. constructor.
    eapply IHt1. exact H1. apply lc_at_mono with (k := k); assumption.
  - constructor.
  - constructor.
Qed.
```

Substitution functions and lemmas:

```coq
(** ** Substitution of free variables *)

Fixpoint tsubst (sigma : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => sigma x
  | tm_app t1 t2 => tm_app (tsubst sigma t1) (tsubst sigma t2)
  | tm_abs T t1 => tm_abs T (tsubst sigma t1)
  | tm_true => tm_true
  | tm_false => tm_false
  end.

Definition extend (x : atom) (u : tm) (sigma : atom -> tm) : atom -> tm :=
  fun y => if Nat.eqb x y then u else sigma y.

Lemma extend_eq : forall x u sigma, extend x u sigma x = u.
Proof. intros. unfold extend. rewrite Nat.eqb_refl. reflexivity. Qed.

Lemma extend_neq : forall x y u sigma, x <> y -> extend x u sigma y = sigma y.
Proof. intros. unfold extend. rewrite Nat.eqb_neq by assumption. reflexivity. Qed.

Lemma tsubst_id : forall t, tsubst (fun x => tm_fvar x) t = t.
Proof.
  induction t; simpl; try reflexivity.
  - rewrite IHt1, IHt2. reflexivity.
  - rewrite IHt. reflexivity.
Qed.
```

Since `try reflexivity` solves bvar, fvar, true, false, the remaining are app and abs. But the bullets then apply to the remaining goals in order: app first, then abs ✓.

Now `tsubst_open_gen`:
```coq
Lemma tsubst_open_gen : forall t k x u sigma,
  ~ In x (fv t) ->
  (forall y, locally_closed (sigma y)) ->
  tsubst (extend x u sigma) (open_rec k (tm_fvar x) t) = open_rec k u (tsubst sigma t).
Proof.
  intro t. induction t; intros k x u sigma Hfresh Hsig; simpl.
  - (* bvar *) destruct (Nat.eqb k n) eqn:E.
    + apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. subst n. simpl. rewrite Nat.eqb_refl. reflexivity.
    + apply Nat.eqb_neq in E. simpl. reflexivity.

    + exact Hsig.

    rewrite (IHt1 (S k) x u sigma).
    + reflexivity.
    + simpl in Hfresh. exact Hfresh.
    + exact Hsig.
  - reflexivity.
  - reflexivity.
Qed.
```

Lemma SN_value : forall t, value t -> strongly_normalizing t.

  intros t Hv. apply SN_intro. intros t' Hstep.

  - exfalso. eapply value_no_step; eassumption.
Qed.

Now `sn_app_value` and `sn_app`. Let me write them with the induction on strongly_normalizing.

```
Lemma sn_app_value : forall (v : tm), strongly_normalizing v ->
  forall (u : tm), value u -> locally_closed v ->
  (forall (T : ty) (body v' : tm),
      multi step u (tm_abs T body) -> multi step v v' -> value v' ->
      strongly_normalizing (open body v')) ->
  strongly_normalizing (tm_app u v).
Proof.
  intros v Hsn. induction Hsn as [v Hredv IH].
  intros u Huval Hlcv Hcompat.
  apply SN_intro. intros t' Hstep.
  inversion Hstep as [T0 body0 w Hlcabs Hvw | t1 t1' t2 Hst1 Hlc2 | v1 t2 t2' Hval1 Hst2]; subst.
  - (* beta *) eapply Hcompat; [apply multi_refl | apply multi_refl | exact Hvw].
  - (* app1: u --> t1' contradicts value u *) exfalso. eapply value_no_step; eassumption.
  - (* app2 *) 
    eapply IH.
    + exact Hst2.
    + exact Huval.
    + eapply step_preserves_lc; eassumption.
    + intros T body v' Hmu Hmv Hvv'. eapply Hcompat; [exact Hmu | | exact Hvv'].
      eapply multi_step; eassumption.
Qed.
```
Wait, careful: in the app1 case (ST_App1), the rule is `t1 --> t1'` with the whole term `tm_app t1 t2`. After subst with our term tm_app u v, we get t1 = u, t2 = v. Hst1 : u --> t1'. And Huval : value u → contradiction ✓ via value_no_step u Huval t1' Hst1.

In the app2 case (ST_App2): v1 = u, t2 = v, t2' = t2'; Hval1 : value u (redundant with Huval), Hst2 : v --> t2'. Goal: SN (tm_app u t2') (after subst). Apply IH: IH : forall y, v --> y -> forall u, value u -> locally_closed y -> compat y -> SN (app u y) — hmm, what's the exact form of IH?

The motive: the goal at the time of `induction Hsn` is `forall u, value u -> locally_closed v -> compat u v -> SN (app u v)`. So the motive P v := forall u, value u -> lc v -> compat u v -> SN (app u v), and the IH is `forall y, v --> y -> P y`, i.e., `forall y, v --> y -> forall u, value u -> lc y -> compat u y -> SN (app u y)`.

So `eapply IH` with y := t2' leaves: v --> t2' (Hst2), then u, value u, lc t2', compat u t2'. ✓ So the bullets in order: Hst2, Huval, lc t2', compat.

`eapply IH. exact Hst2. exact Huval. eapply step_preserves_lc; eassumption. intros ...` ✓ — though `eapply IH` might need the y to be inferred from the goal (SN (app u t2')) ✓.

```coq
Lemma R_app : forall (T1 T2 : ty) (u v : tm),
  R (Ty_Arrow T1 T2) u -> R T1 v -> R T2 (tm_app u v).
Proof.
  intros T1 T2 u v Hu Hv.
  simpl in Hu. destruct Hu as [Hlcu [HSnu Hclu]].
  assert (Hlcv : locally_closed v) by (eapply R_lc; eassumption).
  assert (HSnv : strongly_normalizing v) by (eapply R_sn; eassumption).
  assert (Hsnapp : strongly_normalizing (tm_app u v)).
  { eapply sn_app; [exact Hlcu | exact HSnu | exact Hlcv | exact Hsnv |].
    intros T body v' Hmu Hmv Hvv'.
    assert (Hlcabs : locally_closed (tm_abs T body)).
    { eapply multi_preserves_lc; [exact Hmu | exact Hlcu]. }
    assert (Hvala : value (tm_abs T body)) by (apply v_abs; exact Hlcabs).
    assert (HRv' : R T1 v') by (eapply R_multi; eassumption).
    assert (HRapp : R T2 (tm_app (tm_abs T body) v')) by (eapply Hclu; [exact Hmu | exact Hvala | exact Hvv' | exact HRv']).
    eapply SN_step; [eapply R_sn; exact HRapp |].
    apply ST_AppAbs; assumption. }
  split.
  - constructor; assumption.
  - split; [exact Hsnapp |].
    destruct T2 as [| T2a T2b].
    + (* Bool *) intros z Hmz Hvalz.
      destruct (app_multi_value_inv_gen (tm_app u v) z Hmz Hvalz u v eq_refl)
        as (T & body & v' & Hmu & Hmv & Hvv' & Hmz).
      assert (Hlcabs : locally_closed (tm_abs T body)) by (eapply multi_preserves_lc; [exact Hmu | exact Hlcu]).
      assert (Hvala : value (tm_abs T body)) by (apply v_abs; exact Hlcabs).
      assert (HRv' : R T1 v') by (eapply R_multi; eassumption).
      assert (HRapp : R Ty_Bool (tm_app (tm_abs T body) v')) by (eapply Hclu; [exact Hmu | exact Hvala | exact Hvv' | exact HRv']).
      simpl in HRapp. destruct HRapp as [HlcB [HsnB HclB]].
      eapply HclB; [| exact Hvalz].
      eapply multi_step; [apply ST_AppAbs; assumption | exact Hmz].
    + (* Arrow *) intros z Hmz Hvalz y Hvy HRy.
      ...same...
```

```coq
Definition env_rel (Gamma : context) (sigma : atom -> tm) : Prop :=
  (forall x, locally_closed (sigma x)) /\
  (forall (x : atom) (T : ty), Gamma x = Some T -> R T (sigma x)).

Lemma has_type_lc : forall (Gamma : context) (t : tm) (T : ty),
  has_type Gamma t T -> locally_closed t.
Proof.
  intros Gamma t T H. induction H as [Gamma x T Hlookup
    | L Gamma T1 T2 t1 Hprem IH
    | Gamma t1 t2 T1 T2 Ht1 IH1 Ht2 IH2
    | Gamma | Gamma].
  - apply lc_fvar.
  - apply lc_abs.
    pose proof (IH (fresh (L ++ fv t1)) (fresh_notin_left L (fv t1))) as H0.
    apply lc_open_fvar_inv with (x := fresh (L ++ fv t1)). exact H0.
  - apply lc_app; assumption.
  - apply lc_true.
  - apply lc_false.
Qed.
```
Hmm, the induction pattern for has_type: The constructors:
- T_Var : forall Gamma x T, Gamma x = Some T -> has_type Gamma (tm_fvar x) T. Pattern: Gamma x T Hlookup (no IH since no recursive has_type).
- T_Abs : forall (L : list atom) Gamma T1 T2 t1, (forall x, ~ In x L -> has_type ...) -> has_type ... Pattern: L Gamma T1 T2 t1 Hprem IH.
- T_App : forall Gamma t1 t2 T1 T2, has_type Gamma t1 (T1->T2) -> has_type Gamma t2 T1 -> has_type Gamma (tm_app t1 t2) T2. Pattern: Gamma t1 t2 T1 T2 Ht1 IH1 Ht2 IH2 ✓ (each recursive premise followed by its IH).
- T_True : forall Gamma. Pattern: Gamma.
- T_False : Gamma.

Careful about the interleaving for T_Abs: the premise is a function `forall x, ~ In x L -> has_type ...`; the IH would be `forall x, ~ In x L -> P ...`. In the `as` pattern, does the IH come right after the premise? For T_App it's Ht1 IH1 Ht2 IH2 (interleaved ✓). For T_Abs: the premise Hprem then IH ✓.

Hmm, but for T_Abs there might be an issue: the recursive occurrence is under binders, and Coq's `as` pattern for such nested recursion: I believe it's [L Gamma T1 T2 t1 Hprem IH] where IH has the forall x form. Let's test.

Also, the final theorem:
```coq
Theorem normalization : forall t T,
  <{ empty |-- t \in T }> ->
  strongly_normalizing t.
Proof.
  intros t T Hty.
  assert (Henv : env_rel empty (fun x => tm_fvar x)).
  { split.
    - intros x. apply lc_fvar.
    - intros x T0 Hx. unfold empty in Hx. discriminate Hx. }
  pose proof (fundamental empty t T Hty (fun x => tm_fvar x) Henv) as HR.
  rewrite tsubst_id in HR.
  eapply R_sn. exact HR.
Qed.
```
